[33:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2008s) At this point, you're probably thinking,
[33:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2010s) "Hang on, I thought we were supposed to
[33:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2011s) keep HTML, CSS, and JavaScript in

[33:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2016s) Separation of concerns is a key part of
[33:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2018s) writing well organized code. But let's
[33:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2020s) talk about what that actually means in
[33:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2022s) modern web development and how Vue
[33:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2024s) approaches it. That way of separating
[33:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2027s) file types made a lot of sense when
[33:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2028s) websites were simpler. But as apps have
[33:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2031s) gotten bigger and more dynamic, those
[33:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2033s) language-based files don't always help
[33:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2035s) anymore. In fact, they can get in the
[33:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2037s) way. Imagine this diagram with a ton
[33:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2039s) more files. Imagine how many arrows
[34:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2040s) there would be. This is what Vue states
[34:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2043s) on their own documentation. Separation
[34:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2045s) of concerns is not equal to the
[34:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2047s) separation of file types. In large
[34:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2049s) modern projects, splitting everything by
[34:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2051s) language can actually create more
[34:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2053s) confusion, not less. Vue takes a
[34:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2056s) different approach. Instead of
[34:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2057s) separating by language, it separates by
[34:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2060s) purpose. The component itself is the
[34:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2062s) concern. Each component does one thing.
[34:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2065s) For example, a component renders,
[34:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2067s) styles, and determines the interactivity
[34:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2069s) of, let's say, a footer. And all the
[34:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2071s) code it needs for that footer, the
[34:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2073s) template, logic, and styles, is kept
[34:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2075s) together. This makes your components
[34:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2077s) easier to read, test, reuse, and update.
[34:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2080s) So, while it might look like we're
[34:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2082s) mixing things up, we're actually making
[34:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2083s) our code easier to work with in the long
[34:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2085s) run. We're shifting from separating by
[34:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2087s) file type to separating by units of
[34:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2090s) functionality in our components. And
[34:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kt2E8nblvXU&t=2092s) that mindset is a key part of building
